=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a sequence A = (A_1, A_2, \dots, A_N) of N non-negative integers, and a positive integer M.
Find the following value:
\[
\sum_{1 \leq l \leq r \leq N} \left( \left(\sum_{l \leq i \leq r} A_i\right) \mathbin{\mathrm{mod}} M \right).
\]
Here, X \mathbin{\mathrm{mod}} M denotes the remainder when the non-negative integer X is divided by M.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
A_1 A_2 \dots A_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq M \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 0 \leq A_i \leq 10^9

Sample Input 1

3 4
2 5 0

Sample Output 1

10


- A_1 \mathbin{\mathrm{mod}} M = 2
- (A_1+A_2) \mathbin{\mathrm{mod}} M = 3
- (A_1+A_2+A_3) \mathbin{\mathrm{mod}} M = 3
- A_2 \mathbin{\mathrm{mod}} M = 1
- (A_2+A_3) \mathbin{\mathrm{mod}} M = 1
- A_3 \mathbin{\mathrm{mod}} M = 0

The answer is the sum of these values, 10. Note that the outer sum is not taken modulo M.

Sample Input 2

10 100
320 578 244 604 145 839 156 857 556 400

Sample Output 2

2736

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

import sys

class Fenwick:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n
        self.bit = [0]*(n+1)
    def add(self, idx, val):
        n=self.n; bit=self.bit
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] += val
            idx += idx & -idx
    def sum(self, idx):
        s=0; bit=self.bit
        while idx > 0:
            s += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        return s

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    A = data[2:2+N]
    bit_cnt = Fenwick(M)
    bit_sum = Fenwick(M)
    # add prefix 0
    bit_cnt.add(1, 1)  # value 0 at index1
    # bit_sum.add(1, 0) no need
    total_cnt = 1
    total_sum = 0
    pref = 0
    ans = 0
    for a in A:
        pref = (pref + a) % M
        y = pref
        # query <= y: index y+1
        idx = y + 1
        cnt_le = bit_cnt.sum(idx)
        sum_le = bit_sum.sum(idx)
        cnt_gt = total_cnt - cnt_le
        ans += total_cnt * y - total_sum + cnt_gt * M
        # add current prefix
        bit_cnt.add(idx, 1)
        bit_sum.add(idx, y)
        total_cnt += 1
        total_sum += y
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__": main()

Let's see if code can be made more concise:

import sys

def main():
    it = iter(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = next(it); M = next(it)
    bit_cnt = [0]*(M+1)
    bit_sum = [0]*(M+1)
    def add(bit, idx, val):
        while idx <= M:
            bit[idx] += val
            idx += idx & -idx
    def query(bit, idx):
        s=0
        while idx:
            s += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        return s
    add(bit_cnt, 1, 1)
    total_cnt=1; total_sum=0; pref=0; ans=0
    for _ in range(N):
        a = next(it)
        pref = (pref + a) % M
        idx = pref + 1
        cnt_le = query(bit_cnt, idx)
        sum_le = query(bit_sum, idx)
        ans += total_cnt * pref - total_sum + (total_cnt - cnt_le) * M
        add(bit_cnt, idx, 1)
        add(bit_sum, idx, pref)
        total_cnt += 1
        total_sum += pref
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__": main()

Let's write final code:

import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    it = iter(data)
    N = int(next(it))
    M = int(next(it))
    bit_cnt = [0] * (M + 1)
    bit_sum = [0] * (M + 1)

    def add(bit, idx, val):
        while idx <= M:
            bit[idx] += val
            idx += idx & -idx

    def sum_bit(bit, idx):
        s = 0
        while idx > 0:
            s += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        return s

    add(bit_cnt, 1, 1)
    total_cnt = 1
    total_sum = 0
    pref = 0
    ans = 0

    for _ in range(N):
        a = int(next(it))
        pref = (pref + a) % M
        idx = pref + 1
        cnt_le = sum_bit(bit_cnt, idx)
        sum_le = sum_bit(bit_sum, idx)
        ans += total_cnt * pref - total_sum + (total_cnt - cnt_le) * M
        add(bit_cnt, idx, 1)
        add(bit_sum, idx, pref)
        total_cnt += 1
        total_sum += pref

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's decide final code:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, M = data[0], data[1]

    bit_cnt = [0] * (M + 1)
    bit_sum = [0] * (M + 1)

    def add(bit, idx, val):
        while idx <= M:
            bit[idx] += val
            idx += idx & -idx

    def prefix_sum(bit, idx):
        s = 0
        while idx > 0:
            s += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        return s

    # prefix index 0 has value 0
    add(bit_cnt, 1, 1)

    total_cnt = 1
    total_sum = 0
    pref = 0
    ans = 0

    for i in range(2, 2 + N):
        a = data[i]
        pref = (pref + a) % M
        idx = pref + 1

        cnt_le = prefix_sum(bit_cnt, idx)
        sum_le = prefix_sum(bit_sum, idx)

        ans += total_cnt * pref - total_sum + (total_cnt - cnt_le) * M

        add(bit_cnt, idx, 1)
        add(bit_sum, idx, pref)

        total_cnt += 1
        total_sum += pref

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Wait, `sum_le` variable is computed but not used! In formula ans += total_cnt * pref - total_sum + (total_cnt - cnt_le) * M. We don't need sum_le. Indeed we don't use sum_le. We only need cnt_le and total_sum. Because sum over all previous x is total_sum; no need sum_le separately. Let's verify formula: total = cnt_le*y - sum_le + cnt_gt*y - sum_gt + cnt_gt*M = total_cnt*y - total_sum + cnt_gt*M. Yes, no sum_le. So we don't need bit_sum at all! Wait, is that correct? Let's re-evaluate: For x<=y: contribution y-x. For x>y: contribution y-x+M. Sum over all x = total_cnt*y - total_sum + cnt_gt*M. Yes, indeed we don't need split sum_le. Because y-x term summed over all previous x uses total_sum. The +M only for x>y. So only need count of previous values > y, not their sum. This simplifies to one BIT counts. Great catch! Let's verify with sample1:

So we don't need bit_sum. That halves operations. Nice.

if x<=y: y-x >=0.
if x>y: y-x+M >=1 (since y>=0,x<=M-1 => y-x+M >=1).

Algorithm:
- Maintain BIT counts of prefix modulo values seen so far.
- total_cnt = number of previous prefixes.
- total_sum = sum of previous prefix modulo values.
- For current y:
  cnt_le = number previous x <= y.
  cnt_gt = total_cnt - cnt_le.
  ans += total_cnt * y - total_sum + cnt_gt * M.
- Add y to BIT, total_cnt++, total_sum += y.

Now, code with one BIT:

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    bit = [0]*(M+1)
    def add(idx, val):
        while idx <= M:
            bit[idx] += val
            idx += idx & -idx
    def sum_bit(idx):
        s=0
        while idx >0:
            s += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        return s

    add(1,1)  # prefix 0
    total_cnt=1
    total_sum=0
    pref=0
    ans=0
    for i in range(2, 2+N):
        pref = (pref + data[i]) % M
        idx = pref + 1
        cnt_le = sum_bit(idx)
        cnt_gt = total_cnt - cnt_le
        ans += total_cnt * pref - total_sum + cnt_gt * M
        add(idx, 1)
        total_cnt += 1
        total_sum += pref
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, M = data[0], data[1]

    bit = [0] * (M + 1)

    def add(idx, val):
        while idx <= M:
            bit[idx] += val
            idx += idx & -idx

    def pref_sum(idx):
        s = 0
        while idx > 0:
            s += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        return s

    # prefix sum before any element is 0
    add(1, 1)

    total_cnt = 1
    total_sum = 0
    pref = 0
    ans = 0

    for i in range(2, 2 + N):
        pref = (pref + data[i]) % M
        idx = pref + 1

        cnt_le = pref_sum(idx)
        cnt_gt = total_cnt - cnt_le

        ans += total_cnt * pref - total_sum + cnt_gt * M

        add(idx, 1)
        total_cnt += 1
        total_sum += pref

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Sum over all x:

= cnt_le*y - sum_le + cnt_gt*y - sum_gt + cnt_gt*M
= (cnt_le+cnt_gt)*y - (sum_le+sum_gt) + cnt_gt*M
= total_cnt*y - total_sum + cnt_gt*M.

We can write code with inline Fenwick operations in loop, no helper functions. It might be slightly faster and still readable. Let's draft:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    bit = [0] * (M + 1)

    # add prefix 0
    idx = 1
    while idx <= M:
        bit[idx] += 1
        idx += idx & -idx

    total_cnt = 1
    total_sum = 0
    pref = 0
    ans = 0

    for i in range(2, 2 + N):
        pref = (pref + data[i]) % M
        idx = pref + 1

        # query count <= pref
        s = 0
        j = idx
        while j > 0:
            s += bit[j]
            j -= j & -j
        cnt_le = s

        cnt_gt = total_cnt - cnt_le
        ans += total_cnt * pref - total_sum + cnt_gt * M

        # add current prefix
        j = idx
        while j <= M:
            bit[j] += 1
            j += j & -j

        total_cnt += 1
        total_sum += pref

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This avoids function calls. It's still clear. Let's use this for performance. Need ensure initial add code correct. For M=1, idx=1, while idx<=1: bit[1]+=1; idx=2; stop. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    bit = [0] * (M + 1)

    # Add prefix sum 0
    idx = 1
    while idx <= M:
        bit[idx] += 1
        idx += idx & -idx

    total_cnt = 1
    total_sum = 0
    pref = 0
    ans = 0

    for i in range(2, 2 + N):
        pref = (pref + data[i]) % M
        idx = pref + 1

        # Count previous prefix values <= pref
        s = 0
        j = idx
        while j > 0:
            s += bit[j]
            j -= j & -j
        cnt_le = s

        cnt_gt = total_cnt - cnt_le
        ans += total_cnt * pref - total_sum + cnt_gt * M

        # Add current prefix value
        j = idx
        while j <= M:
            bit[j] += 1
            j += j & -j

        total_cnt += 1
        total_sum += pref

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
